Expected: FAIL. Then create `web/src/lib/sim/compare.ts`:

```ts
import type { Summary } from '../report/types';
import { playerActor } from './sentence';

export interface AbilityDiff {
  name: string;
  simCasts: number;
  actualCasts: number;
  simDamage: number;
  actualDamage: number;
}

export interface AuraDiff {
  name: string;
  simUptimeMs: number;
  actualUptimeMs: number;
}

export interface Comparison {
  headline: string;
  simDps: number;
  actualDps: number;
  /** actual / simulated, clamped to [0, 2] as the contract clamps the stored column. */
  executionScore: number | null;
  abilities: AbilityDiff[];
  auras: AuraDiff[];
  /** The differences in words, largest gap first: at most five abilities and three auras. */
  lines: string[];
}

export const COMPARE_NO_PLAYER = 'That fight has no damage recorded for this character.';
export const MAX_ABILITY_LINES = 5;
export const MAX_AURA_LINES = 3;

function dpsOf(summary: Summary, total: number): number {
  return summary.duration_ms <= 0 ? 0 : (total / summary.duration_ms) * 1000;
}

function castsByName(summary: Summary, guid: string): Map<string, number> {
  const casts = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const row of summary.casts) {
    if (row.guid !== guid) continue;
    casts.set(row.spell_name, (casts.get(row.spell_name) ?? 0) + row.succeeded);
  }
  return casts;
}

function damageByName(abilities: { name: string; total: number }[]): Map<string, number> {
  const damage = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const ability of abilities) damage.set(ability.name, (damage.get(ability.name) ?? 0) + ability.total);
  return damage;
}

function upByName(summary: Summary, guid: string): Map<string, number> {
  const up = new Map<string, number>();
  for (const track of summary.auras) {
    if (track.target_guid !== guid || track.type !== 'BUFF') continue;
    up.set(track.name, Math.max(up.get(track.name) ?? 0, track.uptime_ms));
  }
  return up;
}

function pct(part: number, whole: number): number {
  return whole <= 0 ? 0 : Math.round((part / whole) * 100);
}

function round(value: number): string {
  return Math.round(value).toLocaleString('en-US');
}

/**
 * `actualPlayerName` names the character in the logged fight; the sim has exactly one player,
 * so its actor is whichever did the most damage.
 */
export function compareSummaries(sim: Summary, actual: Summary, actualPlayerName: string): Comparison {
  const simActor = playerActor(sim);
  const actualActor = actual.damage_done.find((row) => row.name === actualPlayerName) ?? null;

  if (simActor === null || actualActor === null) {
    return {
      headline: COMPARE_NO_PLAYER,
      simDps: 0,
      actualDps: 0,
      executionScore: null,
      abilities: [],
      auras: [],
      lines: [],
    };
  }

  const simDps = dpsOf(sim, simActor.total);
  const actualDps = dpsOf(actual, actualActor.total);
  const executionScore = simDps <= 0 ? null : Math.min(2, Math.max(0, actualDps / simDps));

  const simCasts = castsByName(sim, simActor.guid);
  const actualCasts = castsByName(actual, actualActor.guid);
  const simDamage = damageByName(simActor.abilities);
  const actualDamage = damageByName(actualActor.abilities);

  const abilityNames = [...new Set([...simDamage.keys(), ...actualDamage.keys()])];
  const abilities: AbilityDiff[] = abilityNames
    .map((name) => ({
      name,
      simCasts: simCasts.get(name) ?? 0,
      actualCasts: actualCasts.get(name) ?? 0,
      simDamage: simDamage.get(name) ?? 0,
      actualDamage: actualDamage.get(name) ?? 0,
    }))
    .sort((a, b) => Math.max(b.simDamage, b.actualDamage) - Math.max(a.simDamage, a.actualDamage));

  const simUp = upByName(sim, simActor.guid);
  const actualUp = upByName(actual, actualActor.guid);
  const auras: AuraDiff[] = [...new Set([...simUp.keys(), ...actualUp.keys()])]
    .map((name) => ({
      name,
      simUptimeMs: simUp.get(name) ?? 0,
      actualUptimeMs: actualUp.get(name) ?? 0,
    }))
    .sort((a, b) => Math.max(b.simUptimeMs, b.actualUptimeMs) - Math.max(a.simUptimeMs, a.actualUptimeMs));

  const castLines = [...abilities]
    .filter((row) => row.simCasts !== row.actualCasts)
    .sort((a, b) => Math.abs(b.simCasts - b.actualCasts) - Math.abs(a.simCasts - a.actualCasts))
    .slice(0, MAX_ABILITY_LINES)
    .map((row) => `${row.name} cast ${row.actualCasts} times, the sim expects ${row.simCasts}.`);

  const auraLines = [...auras]
    .filter((row) => pct(row.simUptimeMs, sim.duration_ms) !== pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actual.duration_ms))
    .sort(
      (a, b) =>
        Math.abs(
          b.simUptimeMs / Math.max(sim.duration_ms, 1) - b.actualUptimeMs / Math.max(actual.duration_ms, 1),
        ) -
        Math.abs(
          a.simUptimeMs / Math.max(sim.duration_ms, 1) - a.actualUptimeMs / Math.max(actual.duration_ms, 1),
        ),
    )
    .slice(0, MAX_AURA_LINES)
    .map(
      (row) =>
        `${row.name} uptime ${pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actual.duration_ms)}% against ${pct(row.simUptimeMs, sim.duration_ms)}%.`,
    );

  const headline =
    executionScore === null
      ? `This fight did ${round(actualDps)} DPS; the sim produced no damage to compare it with.`
      : `This fight did ${round(actualDps)} DPS; the sim expects ${round(simDps)}. That is ${Math.round(executionScore * 100)}% of what this gear can do.`;

  return {
    headline,
    simDps,
    actualDps,
    executionScore,
    abilities,
    auras,
    lines: [...castLines, ...auraLines],
  };
}
```

Every number is `font-mono tabular`. The full component is:

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/sim/CompareView.svelte -->
<!-- The sim beside the fight. The words come first and the tables second, because most
     people will read "Bloodthirst cast 33 times, the sim expects 41" and stop -- that is
     the answer, and the tables are the working.
     Nothing here is coloured. A red-and-green diff would be a fourth colour vocabulary on
     a page that already has class colours, rarity colours and percentile tokens, and the
     design system does not allow colour to carry meaning on its own anyway. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';
  import type { Comparison } from '../../lib/sim/compare';

  let {
    comparison,
    simDuration,
    actualDuration,
  }: { comparison: Comparison; simDuration: number; actualDuration: number } = $props();

  const pct = (ms: number, of: number): string => (of <= 0 ? '0%' : `${Math.round((ms / of) * 100)}%`);
  const amount = (value: number): string => Math.round(value).toLocaleString('en-US');

  const abilityRow =
    'border-line-soft grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_auto_auto] items-center gap-x-3 gap-y-1 border-b px-3 py-2 text-[14px] last:border-b-0 md:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1.6fr)_72px_72px_88px_88px]';
</script>

<div class="flex flex-col gap-4" data-testid="sim-compare">
  <p class="text-strong px-[18px] text-[15px] md:px-0" role="status" data-testid="compare-headline">
    {comparison.headline}
  </p>

  {#if comparison.lines.length > 0}
    <ul class="flex flex-col gap-1 px-[18px] md:px-0" data-testid="compare-lines">
      {#each comparison.lines as line (line)}
        <li class="text-[14px]">{line}</li>
      {/each}
    </ul>
  {/if}

  <section class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel mx-[18px] border md:mx-0" data-testid="compare-abilities">
    <div class={`${abilityRow} label text-muted hidden md:grid`}>
      <span>{simCopy.compareAbility}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareActualCasts}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimCasts}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareActualDamage}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimDamage}</span>
    </div>
    {#each comparison.abilities as row (row.name)}
      <div class={abilityRow} data-testid={`compare-ability-${row.name}`}>
        <span class="text-strong truncate font-semibold">{row.name}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-right font-mono">{row.actualCasts}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted text-right font-mono">{row.simCasts}</span>
        <span class="tabular hidden text-right font-mono md:inline">{amount(row.actualDamage)}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted hidden text-right font-mono md:inline">{amount(row.simDamage)}</span>
        <span class="text-muted label col-span-3 md:hidden">
          {amount(row.actualDamage)} {simCopy.compareThisFight} · {amount(row.simDamage)} {simCopy.compareSimulated}
        </span>
      </div>
    {/each}
  </section>

  <section class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel mx-[18px] border md:mx-0" data-testid="compare-auras">
    <div class="border-line-soft label text-muted grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_72px_72px] items-center gap-x-3 border-b px-3 py-2">
      <span>{simCopy.compareBuff}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareThisFight}</span>
      <span class="text-right">{simCopy.compareSimulated}</span>
    </div>
    {#each comparison.auras as row (row.name)}
      <div
        class="border-line-soft grid min-h-11 grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_72px_72px] items-center gap-x-3 border-b px-3 py-2 text-[14px] last:border-b-0"
        data-testid={`compare-aura-${row.name}`}
      >
        <span class="text-strong truncate font-semibold">{row.name}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-right font-mono">{pct(row.actualUptimeMs, actualDuration)}</span>
        <span class="tabular text-muted text-right font-mono">{pct(row.simUptimeMs, simDuration)}</span>
      </div>
    {/each}
  </section>

  <p class="text-muted px-[18px] text-[12px] md:px-0" data-testid="compare-footnote">
    {simCopy.compareFootnote}
  </p>
</div>
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Wire it into `SimView.svelte`**

When `state.mode === 'compare'` and `state.ref` parses as a fight ref, `SimView`:

1. calls `store.loadFight(state.ref)` — which also gives it the character;
2. fetches the actual summary once, through `fetchReportMeta` then `fetchSummary`, into local state, showing `simCopy.compareLoading` while it is in flight;
3. runs the sim automatically the moment both the character and the actual summary are in hand — compare mode is the one place a sim starts without a press, because the player already pressed something to get here;
4. renders `CompareView` with `compareSummaries(result.summary, actual, character.name)` in place of the sentence and results.

A failure at step 2 shows `simCopy.fightNoCombatant` and falls back to plain sim mode with the character still loaded, rather than a dead page.

## Task 17: Saved sims — `/sim/<sim_id>` and the history list

Design 4.3: a public, shareable result with an unfurl card, stored like a build; signed-in players keep a history and can name entries.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/components/sim/SavedSim.svelte`, `web/src/components/sim/SimHistory.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`, `web/src/lib/sim/store.svelte.ts`
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/sim-saved.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `fetchSim`, `saveSim`, `listMySims` (Task 2); `SimResult`, `SimListRow` (Task 2); `isStale`, `engineLabel` (Task 1); `settingsLabel`, `encounterLabel` (Task 6); `specLabel` (Task 1); `SimResults` (Task 14); `CharacterStrip` (Task 11).
- Produces:
  - `SavedSim.svelte`: `let { result, onrerun }: { result: SimResult; onrerun: () => void } = $props();`
  - `SimHistory.svelte`: `let { rows, error, onopen }: { rows: SimListRow[] | null; error: string | null; onopen: (simId: string) => void } = $props();`
  - `store.save()` extended to take an optional title; `store.rename(simId, title)` is **not** added — see below.

### Saving, decided

The `Save this sim` button under the results:

1. is disabled until `store.result !== null`;
2. on press, opens a one-field inline form, not a dialog: a text input pre-filled with `settingsLabel(store.settings)` — `Raid-buffed, 3:00, single target` — a `Save` button and a `Cancel` button. Pre-filling means the common case is one more press, and the field is what the contract's `title` column holds;
3. on success, replaces the form with the saved URL in a read-only input and a `Copy link` button, exactly as `SharePanel.svelte` already does for builds. The page does **not** navigate: the player is looking at their result and moving them away from it to show them the same result is a worse page.
4. on failure, shows `simCopy.saveFailed` beside the button and leaves the form open with what they typed.

Renaming a saved sim is **not** built. The contract has no route for it (`POST /v1/sims` and four reads, no `PATCH`), and inventing one is the API lane's call, not this lane's. The design's "can name entries" is satisfied by naming at save time. **Record this in the finish report.**

### `/sim/<sim_id>`, decided

A saved sim is read-only and is not the sim page with a result in it. What renders, top to bottom:

1. The heading row, with the title as the `<h1>` when the sim has one, and `specLabel(spec)` when it does not.
2. A **result header** in place of the run control: one `bg-raised border border-line rounded-panel p-4` panel, `flex flex-wrap items-baseline gap-x-6 gap-y-2`, carrying
   - the figure at `text-[32px] font-mono tabular text-gold` with its `± band`,
   - `encounterLabel(...)` in `text-muted text-[13px]`,
   - `3,000 iterations · 5.1 s · browser` in mono muted,
   - `Engine 7779ebb` as a `pill pill-sample` when stale, plain mono text when current,
   - the saved date.
3. The character strip, from `result.summary.roster[0]` and `result.request.source` — a stored sim carries no gear list, so the strip renders the name, class, spec and source pill and **omits the gear grid**. That is a real limitation of what the contract stores; the strip takes a `gearKnown` boolean and renders one muted line, `simCopy.savedNoGear` — "The gear this was run with is not stored with the result." — instead of an empty grid of seventeen `Empty` slots, which would read as a naked character.
4. `SimResults`, unchanged.
5. Two actions: `Run this yourself`, which opens `/sim` with the request's source and ref in the query so the player can re-run and change things, and `Copy link`.

A stale engine version shows the pill and `simCopy.staleEngine`, and `Run this yourself` is the offered remedy. It is never re-run automatically.

### The history list

On `/sim` for a signed-in player, below the source switcher (so it does not push the switcher off the fold): a `Your sims` panel listing up to 100 rows from `GET /v1/sims?mine=1`, each a `min-h-11` row of `title · specLabel · 1,432 DPS · date`, the whole row a link to `/sim/<id>`. Signed out, the panel does not render at all — not a signed-out placeholder, which would be an advertisement on a page that is otherwise useful without an account.

Add the two actions. Every number is `font-mono tabular`; the date is `result.request.source.captured_at`'s day, or the summary's absence of one is not invented.

- [ ] **Step 2: Extend `CharacterStrip.svelte` for the gearless case**

Add `gearKnown = true` to its props and, when false, render `simCopy.savedNoGear` in `text-muted text-[13px]` where the grid would be. Nothing else changes; the existing tests keep passing because the prop defaults to true.

## Task 18: The signed-in landing state

Design 4.6: "`/sim` for a member shows their characters with last-logout gear and one button per character. No form is shown unless the member opens one."

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/components/sim/LandingState.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/sim/SimView.svelte`, `web/src/components/sim/SourceSwitcher.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/sim-landing.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `fetchMe`, `Me`, `MeCharacter` from `web/src/lib/account/api.ts`; `fromStoredCharacter` (Task 7); `characterKey`, `CharacterPath` from `web/src/lib/characters.ts`; `classColorVar` from `web/src/lib/report/format.ts`.
- Produces: `LandingState.svelte`: `let { characters, busyKey, onpick, onother }: { characters: MeCharacter[]; busyKey: string | null; onpick: (path: CharacterPath) => void; onother: () => void } = $props();`

### Decided, including the limitation

For a signed-in member with characters, `/sim` opens on the landing state **instead of** the source switcher:

- One `bg-raised border border-line rounded-panel` list, one row per character: a 28px class-coloured square, the name in the class colour, a muted `Whitemane · US` descriptor, and a right-aligned `Sim` button at `min-h-11`. The whole row is not the button — the row is a link to `/sim?source=armory&ref=<key>`, and the button is the same action; a member on a phone gets a 44px target either way.
- Below the list, one plain text button: `simCopy.otherCharacter`, "Sim something else" — which opens the source switcher. That is the design's "no form is shown unless the member opens one".
- While a character is loading, its own row's button reads `Loading…` and is disabled; the others stay live.

**The limitation, stated on the page.** Per the contract, Armory is not a source yet: `sim-input` answers from the newest addon export or last ranked fight. So the list's own footnote is `simCopy.landingSourceNote` — *"Gear comes from your last addon export or your last logged fight. Blizzard has no character profile API for Forever yet."* — and each row's source pill, once loaded, says which of the two it actually was, because `fromStoredCharacter` copies the API's answer. The design's phrase "last-logout gear" is exactly what an addon export is, so this is the design honoured through the only source that exists, not a substitute for it.

A member with **no** characters gets the source switcher and one line: `simCopy.noCharactersYet`, *"No characters yet. Install the addon and the companion, or paste an export."* with a link to `/logs`.

`fetchMe` is called once by `SimView` on mount; a null answer is the signed-out state and is not an error.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the component**

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/sim/LandingState.svelte -->
<!-- What a signed-in member sees when they open /sim: their characters, one button each,
     and no form at all until they ask for one.
     The footnote is not boilerplate. The contract's sim-input has no Armory source yet, so
     the gear behind each of these rows is the member's last addon export or last logged
     fight, and a member who assumed the Armory was being read would be wrong about how
     fresh their gear is. The design's "last-logout gear" is precisely what an addon export
     holds, so this is the feature through the source that exists. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import type { MeCharacter } from '../../lib/account/api';
  import type { CharacterPath } from '../../lib/characters';
  import { rulesetLabel } from '../../lib/characters';
  import { classColorVar } from '../../lib/report/format';
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';

  let {
    characters,
    busyKey,
    onpick,
    onother,
  }: {
    characters: MeCharacter[];
    busyKey: string | null;
    onpick: (path: CharacterPath) => void;
    onother: () => void;
  } = $props();

  function pathOf(character: MeCharacter): CharacterPath {
    const [region, ruleset, slug] = character.key.split('/');
    return { region, ruleset, slug } as CharacterPath;
  }
</script>

<section class="mx-[18px] flex flex-col gap-3 md:mx-0" data-testid="sim-landing">
  <h2 class="section-title text-[15px]">{simCopy.yourCharacters}</h2>

  <ul class="border-line bg-raised rounded-panel flex flex-col border">
    {#each characters as character (character.key)}
      {@const colour = classColorVar(character.class)}
      <li
        class="border-line-soft flex min-h-11 flex-wrap items-center gap-3 border-b px-3 py-2 last:border-b-0"
        data-testid={`sim-character-${character.key}`}
      >
        <span class="rounded-control h-7 w-7 shrink-0" style={`background: ${colour}`} aria-hidden="true"></span>
        <span class="text-[15px] font-semibold" style={`color: ${colour}`}>{character.name}</span>
        <span class="text-muted text-[13px]">
          {rulesetLabel(character.ruleset)} · {character.region.toUpperCase()}
        </span>
        <button
          type="button"
          class="border-line-warm-strong rounded-control text-strong label ml-auto min-h-11 border px-4 disabled:opacity-50 md:min-h-9"
          disabled={busyKey !== null}
          onclick={() => onpick(pathOf(character))}
          data-testid={`sim-pick-${character.key}`}
        >
          {busyKey === character.key ? simCopy.loading : simCopy.simIt}
        </button>
      </li>
    {/each}
  </ul>

  <p class="text-muted text-[12px]" data-testid="sim-landing-note">{simCopy.landingSourceNote}</p>

  <button
    type="button"
    class="text-nav label min-h-11 self-start underline md:min-h-9"
    onclick={onother}
    data-testid="sim-other-character"
  >
    {simCopy.otherCharacter}
  </button>
</section>
```

## Task 19: The phone layout audit

Every component in this group was written with its phone layout. This task proves it, the way `report-phone.spec.ts` proves the report's.

**Files:**
- Create: `web/tests/e2e/sim-phone.spec.ts`
- Modify: whichever components the audit catches
- Modify: `web/package.json`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: everything in Group D.
- Produces: `"test:e2e:phone": "playwright test report-phone.spec.ts sim-phone.spec.ts --project=mobile"` in `package.json`.

### What the audit checks

At 360×800, the narrowest viewport the site designs for (`design/Mobile.dc.html`):

1. **No horizontal scroll, anywhere.** `document.documentElement.scrollWidth <= window.innerWidth` on `/sim` empty, `/sim` with a character, `/sim` after a run on every one of the six result tabs, `/sim` in compare mode, `/sim/specs`, and `/sim/simfixture01`.
2. **Every target is 44px.** Every visible `button`, `select`, `a[href]`, `input` and `textarea` not inside a `<p>` has `max(height, width) >= 44`.
3. **The gutter never closes.** Every section's left edge is at `x >= 18` and its right edge at `x <= 342`.
4. **The run control reaches the figure without scrolling.** After loading a character, `sim-run-button` and `sim-dps` are both `toBeInViewport()` — a player on a phone must be able to press run and see the number without moving.
5. **The results tab strip scrolls rather than wraps.** `sim-tab-distribution` is reachable by `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` and the strip's own `scrollWidth` exceeds its client width; the page's does not.
6. **The gear grid is two columns**, checked by comparing the `y` of `sim-slot-head` and `sim-slot-shoulder` (same row) against `sim-slot-neck` (also same row) — head and neck side by side, shoulder on the next row.
7. **Compare's ability rows stack their two figures**, checked by asserting the phone label span inside `compare-ability-Bloodthirst` is visible and the desktop damage cells are not.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the audit**

Create `web/tests/e2e/sim-phone.spec.ts`, modelled on `web/tests/e2e/report-phone.spec.ts` — reuse its helper shape rather than inventing a second one:

```ts
import { expect, test, type Page } from '@playwright/test';

test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' });
test.use({ viewport: { width: 360, height: 800 } });

const GUTTER = 18;
const TARGET = 44;

async function noHorizontalScroll(page: Page): Promise<void> {
  const overflow = await page.evaluate(
    () => document.documentElement.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth,
  );
  expect(overflow, 'page scrolls horizontally').toBeLessThanOrEqual(0);
}

async function targetsAreBigEnough(page: Page): Promise<void> {
  const small = await page.evaluate(
    ({ target }) =>
      [...document.querySelectorAll('button, select, a[href], input, textarea')]
        .filter((element) => {
          const box = element.getBoundingClientRect();
          return box.width > 0 && box.height > 0 && element.closest('p') === null;
        })
        .filter((element) => {
          const box = element.getBoundingClientRect();
          return Math.max(box.height, box.width) < target;
        })
        .map((element) => `${element.tagName}:${element.textContent?.trim().slice(0, 30) ?? ''}`),
    { target: TARGET },
  );
  expect(small, 'hit targets under 44px').toEqual([]);
}
```

then one test per page state calling both helpers, plus the five specific checks above.

# Group F — the planner surfaces

Tasks 20 and 21, in order. They depend on groups A and B, and on nothing in C, D or E: the planner is a different page with a different island.

## Task 20: Live DPS in the planner

Design 4.6: "A talent or slot change in the planner runs a 500-iteration sim in the browser pool and updates a DPS estimate with its error in under a second; a 'Sim this build' control opens the full results."

**Files:**
- Create: `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`, `web/src/components/planner/PlannerDps.svelte`
- Modify: `web/src/components/planner/Planner.svelte`, `web/src/components/planner/SummaryBar.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.test.ts`, `web/tests/e2e/planner-dps.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `PlannerStore` from `web/src/lib/planner/store.svelte.ts`; `createPool`, `SimPool` (Task 4); `runSim`, `SimRunError` (Task 5); `specOf`, `SimCharacter` (Task 6); `ITERATIONS` (Task 2); `confidenceBand` (Task 4); `defaultSettings` (Task 6).
- Produces:
  - `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`: `const LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 350`, `interface LiveDps { readonly state: 'off' | 'pending' | 'running' | 'ready' | 'error'; readonly estimate: Estimate; readonly message: string | null; request(character: SimCharacter | null): void; dispose(): void }`, `function createLiveDps(init?: { pool?: SimPool; debounceMs?: number; now?: () => number }): LiveDps`, `function characterFromPlanner(store: PlannerStore): SimCharacter | null`.
  - `PlannerDps.svelte`: `let { live, href }: { live: LiveDps; href: string } = $props();`

### The rule that protects the planner's Lighthouse budget

`/planner.html` has a performance budget of 0.90 and a total-blocking-time budget of 100 ms in `lighthouserc.json`. A 4 MB wasm module fetched on page load would end both. So:

- **The pool is created on the planner's first edit, never on mount.** `createLiveDps` holds `pool: SimPool | null` and calls `createPool` inside `request()`, not in the factory.
- **Nothing in the planner's module graph imports `engine.ts` statically.** `live-dps.svelte.ts` imports `runSim` and `createPool`; `createPool`'s worker is `new Worker(new URL('./sim.worker.ts', …))`, which Vite emits as its own chunk, and `sim.worker.ts` is the only file that imports `engine.ts`. Task 22 asserts this by checking that `dist/planner-island.js` does not grow past its 60 KB budget, which it would if the engine were inlined.
- **A change while a sim is running cancels it.** Every edit calls `request()`; `request()` clears the pending timer, cancels the in-flight handle, and schedules a new run 350 ms later. Without the cancel, a player dragging through five talents would queue five 500-iteration runs and the pool would be busy for seconds.

350 ms is the debounce: long enough that a burst of clicks is one run, short enough that a deliberate single change feels immediate. 500 iterations at the measured 155 iterations per second per worker is about 0.8 s on a four-worker laptop, which is the design's "under a second".

### The display, decided

A fourth figure in `SummaryBar.svelte`, beside Level, Split and Points, in exactly the same shape — `label text-muted` caption, `font-mono tabular text-[20px] leading-11` value:

| State | Caption | Value |
|---|---|---|
| `off` (no engine yet, no edit made) | `DPS` | `—` in `text-muted` |
| `pending` (debouncing) | `DPS` | the last figure, dimmed to `text-muted` |
| `running` | `DPS` | the last figure, dimmed |
| `ready` | `DPS` | the figure in `text-gold`, with `± band` in `text-muted text-[12px]` under it |
| `error` | `DPS` | `—`, and the message in the bar's existing refusal line |

The figure is never blanked while a new one is computed: a number that vanishes and reappears on every click is worse than a stale number that dims. That is the whole design decision here.

Beside it, a `Sim this build` link at `min-h-11`, to `/sim?source=build&ref=<id>` when the build has been saved, and to `/sim` with the FS1 code in `?code=` otherwise — using the planner's own `encodeFS1`, so no new format. The link is always present, so a player can reach the full results whether or not the estimate has run.

An unsupported spec shows `—` and keeps the link. The planner does **not** fetch `/v1/specs` to find out which specs those are: a fetch on the planner's critical path is exactly what its Lighthouse budget forbids, and the engine is the authority anyway — a spec it has no model for fails the run, `live.state` becomes `error`, and `simCopy.liveDpsFailed` goes in the summary bar's existing refusal line. The `Sim this build` link is still there, and `/sim` has the full spec card for anyone who wants the reason.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.test.ts`:

```ts
// @vitest-environment jsdom
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { createFakeEngine } from '../../fixtures/sim/engine-fake';
import { decodeWireProgress } from '../../fixtures/sim/wire';
import { createPool, type FromWorker, type PoolWorker, type ToWorker } from '../sim/worker';
import type { SimCharacter } from '../sim/character';
import { LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS, createLiveDps } from './live-dps.svelte';

const character: SimCharacter = {
  name: 'Thrallgar',
  spec: 'warrior-fury',
  class_slug: 'warrior',
  race_slug: 'orc',
  level: 60,
  tree_version: '1.15.9.69722',
  point_order: [2001, 2002],
  gear: { head: 12640 },
  buffs: [],
  consumables: [],
  source: { kind: 'manual', ref: '', captured_at: '2026-09-14T10:00:00Z' },
};

function fakeWorker(): PoolWorker {
  const engine = createFakeEngine({ tickMs: 0, ticks: 2 });
  const listeners: ((event: { data: FromWorker }) => void)[] = [];
  const emit = (data: FromWorker): void => listeners.forEach((listen) => listen({ data }));
  return {
    addEventListener: (_type, listener) => listeners.push(listener),
    terminate: () => {},
    postMessage(message: ToWorker) {
      void (async () => {
        if (message.kind === 'abort') return engine.abortById(message.runId);
        const token = message.token;
        try {
          if (message.kind === 'split') {
            emit({ kind: 'many', token, results: engine.raidSimRequestSplit(message.request, message.shards) });
          } else if (message.kind === 'combine') {
            emit({ kind: 'one', token, result: engine.raidSimResultCombination(message.results) });
          } else if (message.kind === 'stats') {
            emit({ kind: 'one', token, result: engine.computeStats(message.request) });
          } else {
            const result = await engine.raidSimAsync(
              message.request,
              (bytes) => emit({ kind: 'progress', token, ...decodeWireProgress(bytes) }),
              message.runId,
            );
            emit({ kind: 'one', token, result });
          }
        } catch (error) {
          emit({ kind: 'failed', token, message: (error as Error).message });
        }
      })();
    },
  };
}

const live = () => createLiveDps({ pool: createPool({ hardwareConcurrency: 2, spawn: fakeWorker }) });

afterEach(() => vi.useRealTimers());

describe('createLiveDps', () => {
  it('is off until something is requested', () => {
    const dps = live();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('off');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBe(0);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('debounces a burst of edits into one run', async () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character);
    dps.request(character);
    dps.request(character);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS - 1);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(50);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('ready');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('runs 500 iterations, which is what "under a second" buys', async () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.iterationsRun).toBe(500);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('keeps the last figure while the next one is computed', async () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(character);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    const first = dps.estimate.mean;
    dps.request({ ...character, gear: { head: 12641 } });
    expect(dps.state).toBe('pending');
    expect(dps.estimate.mean).toBe(first);
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('goes off, not to an error, when there is no character to sim', () => {
    const dps = live();
    dps.request(null);
    expect(dps.state).toBe('off');
    dps.dispose();
  });

  it('says so rather than throwing when the engine fails', async () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    let emit: (data: FromWorker) => void = () => {};
    const broken: PoolWorker = {
      addEventListener: (_type, listener) => {
        emit = (data) => listener({ data });
      },
      terminate: () => {},
      postMessage: (message: ToWorker) => {
        if (message.kind === 'abort') return;
        emit({ kind: 'failed', token: message.token, message: 'wasm trap' });
      },
    };
    const dps = createLiveDps({ pool: createPool({ hardwareConcurrency: 1, spawn: () => broken }) });
    dps.request(character);
    await vi.runAllTimersAsync();
    expect(dps.state).toBe('error');
    expect(dps.message).not.toBeNull();
    dps.dispose();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to see it fail, then write the module**


Expected: FAIL. Then create `web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts`:

```ts
// web/src/lib/planner/live-dps.svelte.ts
// A 500-iteration sim behind every planner edit, and the three rules that make it usable.
//
//   1. The pool is created on the first request, never in this factory. /planner.html has a
//      0.90 performance budget and a 100 ms total-blocking-time budget in lighthouserc.json;
//      a 4 MB wasm module on page load ends both. Nothing on the planner's critical path may
//      reach engine.ts, which is why sim.worker.ts -- a separate Vite chunk -- is the only
//      file that imports it.
//   2. A burst of edits is one run. Clicking through five talents must not queue five runs.
//   3. The last figure stays on screen, dimmed, while the next one is computed. A number
//      that blanks and reappears on every click is worse than a stale number that dims.
import { EMPTY_ESTIMATE } from '../sim/estimate';
import { runSim, SimRunError, type RunHandle } from '../sim/run';
import { defaultSettings } from '../sim/settings';
import type { SimCharacter } from '../sim/character';
import { simCopy } from '../sim/copy';
import { ITERATIONS, type Estimate } from '../sim/types';
import { createPool, type SimPool } from '../sim/worker';

export const LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 350;

export type LiveState = 'off' | 'pending' | 'running' | 'ready' | 'error';

export function createLiveDps(init: { pool?: SimPool; debounceMs?: number } = {}) {
  const debounceMs = init.debounceMs ?? LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS;

  let state = $state<LiveState>('off');
  let estimate = $state<Estimate>(EMPTY_ESTIMATE);
  let iterationsRun = $state(0);
  let message = $state<string | null>(null);

  let pool: SimPool | null = init.pool ?? null;
  let handle: RunHandle | null = null;
  let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

  function poolOnce(): SimPool {
    pool ??= createPool({});
    return pool;
  }

  async function start(character: SimCharacter): Promise<void> {
    state = 'running';
    const settings = defaultSettings();
    handle = runSim(
      poolOnce(),
      {
        spec: character.spec,
        source: character.source,
        raw: {
          spec: character.spec,
          race: character.race_slug,
          level: character.level,
          gear: character.gear as Record<string, number>,
          talents: character.point_order,
          buffs: settings.buffs,
          consumables: settings.consumables,
          encounter: settings.encounter,
          iterations: ITERATIONS.live,
          randomSeed: 0,
        },
      },
      () => {
        // The planner shows one figure at the end, not a ticking one: a number that moves
        // while a player is reading their talent tree is noise, and 500 iterations is fast
        // enough that there is nothing to fill.
      },
    );

    try {
      const result = await handle.result;
      estimate = result.dps;
      iterationsRun = result.iterations_run;
      message = null;
      state = 'ready';
    } catch (error) {
      // A cancel is this module's own doing -- a newer edit arrived -- and is not a failure
      // the player should be told about; the newer run will set the state.
      if (error instanceof SimRunError && error.cancelled) return;
      message = simCopy.liveDpsFailed;
      state = 'error';
    } finally {
      handle = null;
    }
  }

  return {
    get state() {
      return state;
    },
    get estimate() {
      return estimate;
    },
    get iterationsRun() {
      return iterationsRun;
    },
    get message() {
      return message;
    },

    request(character: SimCharacter | null): void {
      if (timer !== null) clearTimeout(timer);
      handle?.cancel();
      if (character === null) {
        state = 'off';
        return;
      }
      state = 'pending';
      timer = setTimeout(() => {
        timer = null;
        void start(character);
      }, debounceMs);
    },

    dispose(): void {
      if (timer !== null) clearTimeout(timer);
      timer = null;
      handle?.cancel();
      pool?.terminate();
      pool = null;
    },
  };
}

export type LiveDps = ReturnType<typeof createLiveDps>;
```

and add `liveDpsFailed: 'DPS estimate unavailable for this build.'` to `simCopy`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write `characterFromPlanner`**

Add to `web/src/lib/sim/character.ts` — it belongs with the other conversions, not in the planner:

```ts
/**
 * The planner's live state as a character. It is `fromBuildDraft` over `store.toDraft()`,
 * so there is exactly one conversion in the codebase and the sim cannot disagree with the
 * planner about what a build is. Null while the planner's data is still loading.
 */
export function characterFromPlanner(store: PlannerStore): SimCharacter | null {
  if (store.talents === null || store.classes.length === 0) return null;
  return fromBuildDraft(store.toDraft(), store.talents, store.classes, store.races, {
    name: store.classRow?.name,
    source: { kind: 'manual', ref: '', captured_at: new Date().toISOString() },
  });
}
```

```svelte
<!-- web/src/components/planner/PlannerDps.svelte -->
<!-- The fourth figure in the summary bar. It is the planner's own shape -- label caption,
     mono value at the same size -- so it reads as one more fact about the build rather
     than a widget bolted on.
     It dims rather than blanks while a new estimate runs: a number that disappears on every
     click is worse than one that is briefly a click behind. -->
<script lang="ts">
  import { confidenceBand } from '../../lib/sim/estimate';
  import { simCopy } from '../../lib/sim/copy';
  import type { LiveDps } from '../../lib/planner/live-dps.svelte';

  let { live, href }: { live: LiveDps; href: string } = $props();

  const stale = $derived(live.state === 'pending' || live.state === 'running');
  const figure = $derived(
    live.estimate.mean > 0 ? Math.round(live.estimate.mean).toLocaleString('en-US') : '—',
  );
  const band = $derived(
    live.state === 'ready' && live.estimate.error > 0
      ? `± ${Math.round(confidenceBand(live.estimate)).toLocaleString('en-US')}`
      : '',
  );
</script>

<div class="flex flex-col gap-1">
  <span class="label text-muted">{simCopy.plannerDpsLabel}</span>
  <span
    class={`tabular font-mono text-[20px] leading-11 ${stale || live.estimate.mean === 0 ? 'text-muted' : 'text-gold'}`}
    data-testid="planner-dps"
  >
    {figure}
  </span>
  {#if band !== ''}
    <span class="tabular text-muted font-mono text-[12px]" data-testid="planner-dps-error">{band}</span>
  {/if}
</div>

<a class="label text-nav flex min-h-11 items-center underline md:min-h-9" {href} data-testid="planner-sim-link">
  {simCopy.simThisBuild}
</a>
```

## Task 21: Build unfurls carrying simmed DPS

Design 4.7: "A shared planner link's card carries the build's simmed DPS and the engine version it was simmed on."

**Files:**
- Modify: `web/src/components/planner/SharePanel.svelte`, `web/src/lib/sim/copy.ts`
- Test: `web/tests/e2e/planner-share-sim.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `saveBuild`, `SavedBuild` from `web/src/lib/planner/share.ts`; `saveSim` (Task 2); `LiveDps` (Task 20); `characterFromPlanner` (Task 20); `runSim` (Task 5); `buildSimRequest` (Task 5); `ITERATIONS` (Task 2).
- Produces: no new module. `SharePanel.svelte` gains `live` and `store` props and one extra step in its save flow.

### The ruling this task makes, and why it changes no contract

The card is rendered by the API. The design says it carries the build's simmed DPS; the contract adds no field to `BuildDraft` and no field to the card route. Rather than invent one:

**When a build is saved and the planner has a live estimate for exactly that build, the share panel also saves a sim for it: a 3,000-iteration browser run posted to `POST /v1/sims` with `source: { kind: 'build', ref: <the new build id>, captured_at: <now> }`.** The API's card renderer then finds the newest sim whose `source.kind` is `build` and whose `source.ref` is that build id, and puts its `dps.mean` and `engine_version` on the card. That is a join over two shapes the contract already defines, and it needs no new field anywhere. **Record it in the finish report as a cross-lane dependency: the API lane owns the card side.**

Three consequences the panel must handle, and does:

1. The 500-iteration live estimate is **not** what is saved. It is a rough figure for a planner; a card is a public claim. The panel runs a fresh 3,000-iteration sim, which is why saving a build with a sim takes a few seconds and the button says so.
2. The sim is saved **after** the build, because it needs the build's id.
3. A failed sim never fails the save. The build is already saved and its link already works; the panel shows `simCopy.buildSimSkipped` and the card simply carries no DPS. Losing a share because a background sim failed would be the worst possible trade.

### The panel, decided

Under the existing `Save and share` button, one checkbox: `Include a simmed DPS on the card`, checked by default when the planner's live estimate is `ready` and the spec is supported, unchecked and disabled otherwise with the title `simCopy.buildSimUnavailable`.

While the sim runs after a save, the share URL is already shown and a muted line under it reads `simCopy.buildSimRunning` — "Simming this build for the card…". It becomes `simCopy.buildSimDone` — "The card will show 1,432 DPS on engine 7779ebb." — or `simCopy.buildSimSkipped` — "The card will not show a DPS figure; the sim did not finish." The link is usable throughout.

Add `live: LiveDps` and `store: PlannerStore` to its props, the checkbox described above, and after a successful `saveBuild`:

```ts
  async function attachSim(build: SavedBuild): Promise<void> {
    const character = characterFromPlanner(store);
    if (!includeSim || character === null) return;
    cardState = 'running';
    try {
      const result = await runSim(poolOnce(), {
        spec: character.spec,
        // The saved build is what the card is for, so that is the sim's source and the id
        // the API's card renderer joins on. No new field on either shape.
        source: { kind: 'build', ref: build.id, captured_at: new Date().toISOString() },
        raw: { ...rawFor(character), iterations: ITERATIONS.normal, randomSeed: 0 },
      }, () => {}).result;
      await saveSim(result);
      cardDps = Math.round(result.dps.mean).toLocaleString('en-US');
      cardVersion = result.engine_version;
      cardState = 'done';
    } catch {
      // The build is saved and its link works. A background sim is not worth losing that.
      cardState = 'skipped';
    }
  }
```

`/sim.html` keeps the **full** 1.6 s LCP budget rather than the planner's relaxed one. That is the point of loading the engine after first paint and creating the pool on the first run: the page's own paint is a heading, a panel and four cards, and it must be as fast as a content page. If it is not, the fix is in the page, not the budget.

`/sim/simfixture01.html` is deliberately **not** in the list. It inlines a full result and mounts six report components on load; it is a shared artefact people open from a link, not a page in the site's hot path, and holding it to the same LCP as `/sim` would be measuring the report components' budget a second time.

# Rulings, and what to put in the finish report

The contract says: "Never change a shape here to resolve a local conflict: rule, record the ruling, and note the friction in the finish report." These are this lane's rulings. Every one of them is implemented in the task named, and every one belongs in the finish report.

| # | The gap | The ruling | Task | Who else it touches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The contract does not say how the browser produces a `summary.Summary`.** It names one adapter, `sim/adapter.Summarize`, which is Go and runs on the server. The browser cannot call it, cannot post the result to the API (the browser lane's promise is that nothing leaves the device), and cannot ask the engine (five entrypoints, "no others"). | `web/src/lib/sim/summarize.ts` is the TypeScript sibling, implementing the contract's own mapping table. A golden test pins it against `sim/adapter`'s checked-in testdata when the engine lands, so the two cannot drift. | 5 | engine, api |
| 2 | **The contract does not say how the web encodes `Raw`.** It says `Raw` is "the engine's own `RaidSimRequest` protobuf, built by the web", but the five allowed wasm entrypoints include no encoder and no TypeScript bindings are published. | `web/src/lib/sim/raw.ts` is the one function that encodes, with the fake's wire format today and one branch to switch. **The engine lane must publish generated TypeScript protobuf bindings alongside `sim.js`, or a sixth entrypoint that accepts a JSON request.** This is the single hardest blocker on this lane and should go to the engine lane now, not in October. | 3 | engine |
| 3 | **Armory is not a character source.** Per the contract's updated `sim-input` row, nothing stores an Armory refresh; the API answers from the newest addon export or last ranked fight. | Three working sources at launch. `fromStoredCharacter` copies whichever source the API reports rather than naming one, so nothing changes here the day Armory lands. The switcher keeps an account card whose copy says where the gear actually comes from, and the landing state carries the same note. **A launch limitation, not a permanent one.** | 7, 11, 18 | api |
| 4 | **`data/curated/specs.json` belongs to the data lane and does not exist.** Nothing may hardcode a spec list. | This lane writes the file and the generator; the data lane owns it from the moment it lands, and the content is the contract's own shape so the merge is trivial. | 1 | data |
| 5 | **A saved sim has no rendered card.** The API draws one per report and per build; a third renderer is not this lane's work. | `/sim/<id>`'s unfurl uses the site's own card, as the rankings and character shells already do. **Deferred to the API lane.** | 9 | api |
| 6 | **A build's card cannot carry a simmed DPS without a new field.** The design asks for one; the contract adds no field to `BuildDraft` or to the card route. | Saving a build also saves a sim whose `source` is `{ kind: 'build', ref: <build id> }`. The API's card renderer joins on that. No new field on either shape. **The API lane owns the card side.** | 21 | api |
| 7 | **There is no rename route for a saved sim.** The design says signed-in players "can name entries"; the contract has `POST /v1/sims` and four reads. | Named at save time, pre-filled with the settings line. No rename. | 17 | api |
| 8 | **Premium is not readable from the web.** The contract puts it on `users.premium`; `GET /v1/me`'s response, as the report lane mirrors it, carries no such field. | `store.premium` stays false and "Run on our servers" never renders. **The API lane needs to add `premium` to `/v1/me`** before the server lane can be reached from the page at all. | 13, 18 | api |
| 9 | **A stored `SimResult` carries no gear list.** `/sim/<id>` therefore cannot draw the slot grid. | The strip says so in one line rather than rendering seventeen `Empty` slots. | 17 | — |
| 10 | **`raidSimResultCombination` and `computeStats` are wasm exports, and the main thread holds no instance.** The design says partials are "combined on the main thread". | The pool routes both to worker 0; `run.ts` still owns what is combined and when, which is the ownership the contract's Web section actually specifies. Loading a second 4 MB module on the main thread would defeat the LCP budget for nothing. | 4 | — |
| 11 | **`phase-3-web` is red on its own `Base.test.ts`.** `Base.astro` carries `data-og="title"`; the test still asserts `<title>`. | Fixed here, in the merge task, because this is the tree that has to be green. | 1 | web (report lane) |
| 12 | **The engine's throughput is unmeasured in this repository.** Every timing budget in this plan — first estimate within 1 s, final within 8 s, planner live DPS under a second — rests on the design's measured 155 iterations per second per worker, against a fake that returns instantly. | The budgets are written into the plan and asserted only where they can be: iteration counts, the 500-iteration live run, the pool size. **The first real `sim.wasm` must be timed against them, and Task 22 re-run, before launch.** | 4, 5, 20, 22 | engine |

---

## Execution notes

- Task 1 and Task 2 are the base and run first, in that order.
- Then groups **A** (3, 4, 5), **B** (6, 7), **C** (8, 9) and **E** (10) may run at the same time, in four worktrees or four subagents.
- Group **F** (20, 21) may start as soon as A and B are both finished; it does not wait for C, D or E.
- Group **D** (11–19) runs strictly in order after A, B and C.
- Task 22 runs last, alone, and is the only task that runs Lighthouse.
